Shared memory per block: - Q tile: Br * D * 2 bytes = 64 * 128 * 2 = 16,384 bytes = 16 KB. - K tile: Bc * D * 2 bytes = 64 * 128 * 2 = 16 KB. - V tile: Bc * D * 2 bytes = 16 KB. - S tile (scores): Br * Bc * 4 bytes (float) = 64 * 64 * 4 = 16 KB. (If we keep in registers/shared) Total: ~64 KB, well under 227KB limit. But we can do without S tile in shared memory by computing dot products directly and doing online softmax, like FlashAttention. That reduces shared memory and register pressure. FlashAttention algorithm: - Load Q chunk (Br x D) into SRAM. - Initialize output accumulator O (Br x D) to zero, running max m (Br) to -inf, running sum l (Br) to 0. - Loop over KV chunks: - Load K chunk (Bc x D) and V chunk (Bc x D) into SRAM. - Compute S_ij = Q_i @ K_j^T (Br x Bc). Each thread computes a fragment. - For each row, find max of S_ij, update running max, subtract from S_ij, exp, multiply by previous sum factor, accumulate to O. - Write O back. Implementing this efficiently in CUDA is non-trivial but manageable for small sizes. Online softmax per row: - For each row i in [0, Br), we need to track: - m_i: running max of S_i across all KV tiles processed so far. - l_i: running sum of exp(S_i - m_i). - O_i: running weighted sum of V. Where V_j is the V tile [Bc, D]. Let's detail the algorithm for each block: Inputs per block: - batch_idx = blockIdx.x / num_heads - head_idx = blockIdx.x % num_heads - q_tile_idx = blockIdx.y - q_start = q_tile_idx * Br // 64 We need to map head_idx to kv_head_idx: `kv_head_idx = head_idx / G`, where `G = num_heads / num_kv_heads`. Since K and V are shaped [B, Hkv, L, D], all query heads in the same group share the same K and V. Shared memory layout: - `half Q_smem[Br][D];` // 64*128*2 = 16KB - `half KV_smem[Bc][D];` // 64*128*2 = 16KB. Reused for K and V. - `float S_smem[Br][Bc];` // 64*64*4 = 16KB. Stores scores for the current KV tile. Total = 48KB. We can also avoid S_smem by computing scores and immediately applying softmax, but then we need to store P for the GEMM with V. We can store P in S_smem (reuse as float). That's fine. Step 1: Load Q tile into Q_smem. - Q global memory: `q[batch_idx, head_idx, q_start + row, d]`. - Each thread loads (Br * D) / 128 = 64 halfs = 128 bytes. Specifically, each thread can load 8 `half4` (8 bytes) = 64 halfs. - We can assign each thread a linear index and load from global memory to Q_smem. Let's write the full Python model with `torch.utils.cpp_extension.load_inline`. The CUDA code will be string inside Python. We need to include `<cuda_fp16.h>`, `<cuda_runtime.h>`, `<math_constants.h>`. Let's write the kernel. We'll call it `fused_gqa_kernel`. We might also need a host function to launch it. One concern: PyTorch's `load_inline` uses `nvcc`. The code must be C++ compatible. Let's draft the code. ```cpp #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <math.h> #include <cstdint> #define WARP_SIZE 32 inline __device__ half4 make_half4(half x, half y, half z, half w) { half4 r; r.x = x; r.y = y; r.z = z; r.w = w; return r; } template <int D, int Br, int Bc> __global__ __launch_bounds__(128) void fused_gqa_kernel( const half* __restrict__ q, const half* __restrict__ k, const half* __restrict__ v, half* __restrict__ out, int batch, int num_heads, int num_kv_heads, int seq_len, float scale) { // grid: (batch * num_heads, (seq_len + Br - 1) / Br) int b = blockIdx.x / num_heads; int hq = blockIdx.x % num_heads; int tile_q = blockIdx.y; int q_start = tile_q * Br; int kv_head = hq / (num_heads / num_kv_heads); int gqa_group = num_heads / num_kv_heads; // base pointers const half* q_ptr = q + ((b * num_heads + hq) * seq_len + q_start) * D; const half* k_ptr = k + ((b * num_kv_heads + kv_head) * seq_len) * D; const half* v_ptr = v + ((b * num_kv_heads + kv_head) * seq_len) * D; half* out_ptr = out + ((b * num_heads + hq) * seq_len + q_start) * D; // shared memory __shared__ half Q_smem[Br * D]; __shared__ half KV_smem[Bc * D]; __shared__ float S_smem[Br * Bc]; __shared__ float smem_alpha[Br]; __shared__ float smem_m[Br]; __shared__ float smem_l[Br]; int tid = threadIdx.x; // Load Q tile int total_q = Br * D; for (int idx = tid; idx < total_q / 4; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (D / 4); int c = idx % (D / 4); half4 val; if (q_start + r < seq_len) { val = reinterpret_cast<const half4*>(q_ptr + r * D)[c]; } else { val = make_half4(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f)); } reinterpret_cast<half4*>(Q_smem + r * D)[c] = val; } __syncthreads(); int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE; int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE; int row = warp_id * (Br / 4) + lane / 2; // Br must be divisible by 4 int d_start = (lane % 2) * (D / 2); bool is_leader = (lane % 2 == 0); // initialize accumulators float o_reg[D/2]; for (int d = 0; d < D/2; ++d) o_reg[d] = 0.0f; float m = -INFINITY; float l = 0.0f; int num_kv_tiles = (seq_len + Bc - 1) / Bc; for (int tile_kv = 0; tile_kv < num_kv_tiles; ++tile_kv) { int kv_start = tile_kv * Bc; // Load K tile // zero-out smem first for oob for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D / 4; idx += blockDim.x) { reinterpret_cast<half4*>(KV_smem)[idx] = make_half4(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f)); } __syncthreads(); for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D / 4; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (D / 4); int c = idx % (D / 4); if (kv_start + r < seq_len) { half4 val = reinterpret_cast<const half4*>(k_ptr + (kv_start + r) * D)[c]; reinterpret_cast<half4*>(KV_smem + r * D)[c] = val; } } __syncthreads(); if (row < Br && q_start + row < seq_len) { // compute scores for this row and tile for (int j = 0; j < Bc; ++j) { float acc = 0.0f; // pointer to Q row and K row const half* q_row = Q_smem + row * D + d_start; const half* k_row = KV_smem + j * D + d_start; #pragma unroll 16 for (int d = 0; d < D/2; d += 4) { half4 q4 = reinterpret_cast<const half4*>(q_row + d)[0]; half4 k4 = reinterpret_cast<const half4*>(k_row + d)[0]; acc += __half2float(q4.x) * __half2float(k4.x); acc += __half2float(q4.y) * __half2float(k4.y); acc += __half2float(q4.z) * __half2float(k4.z); acc += __half2float(q4.w) * __half2float(k4.w); } // reduce partial sum between the two threads in the pair float other = __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, acc, 1); acc += other; if (is_leader) { S_smem[row * Bc + j] = acc * scale; } } } __syncthreads(); // softmax for row if (row < Br && q_start + row < seq_len && is_leader) { float m_ij = -INFINITY; #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < Bc; ++j) { float s = S_smem[row * Bc + j]; if (s > m_ij) m_ij = s; } float m_new = fmaxf(m, m_ij); float alpha = expf(m - m_new); smem_alpha[row] = alpha; smem_m[row] = m_new; float l_new = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < Bc; ++j) { float p = expf(S_smem[row * Bc + j] - m_new); S_smem[row * Bc + j] = p; l_new += p; } l = l * alpha + l_new; smem_l[row] = l; m = m_new; } __syncthreads(); // scale O and update m, l for all threads if (row < Br && q_start + row < seq_len) { float alpha = smem_alpha[row]; } } __syncthreads(); // O += P @ V_chunk if (row < Br && q_start + row < seq_len) { for (int j = 0; j < Bc; ++j) { float p = S_smem[row * Bc + j]; const half* v_row = KV_smem + j * D + d_start; #pragma unroll 16 for (int d = 0; d < D/2; d += 4) { half4 v4 = reinterpret_cast<const half4*>(v_row + d)[0]; o_reg[d+0] += p * __half2float(v4.x); o_reg[d+1] += p * __half2float(v4.y); o_reg[d+2] += p * __half2float(v4.z); o_reg[d+3] += p * __half2float(v4.w); } } } __syncthreads(); } // finalize and write output if (row < Br && q_start + row < seq_len) { float inv_l = 1.0f / smem_l[row]; half* out_row = out_ptr + row * D + d_start; #pragma unroll 16 for (int d = 0; } } } ``` Wait, `smem_l[row]` is written by the leader each tile. Let's draft the CUDA source string fully. ```cpp #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <math.h> #include <cstdint> #define WARP_SIZE 32 struct alignas(8) Half4 { half2 x; int hq = blockIdx.x % num_heads; int tile_q = blockIdx.y; int q_start = tile_q * Br; int kv_head = hq / (num_heads / num_kv_heads); const half* q_ptr = q + ((b * num_heads + hq) * seq_len + q_start) * D; const half* k_ptr = k + ((b * num_kv_heads + kv_head) * seq_len) * D; const half* v_ptr = v + ((b * num_kv_heads + kv_head) * seq_len) * D; half* out_ptr = out + ((b * num_heads + hq) * seq_len + q_start) * D; __shared__ half Q_smem[Br * D]; __shared__ half KV_smem[Bc * D]; __shared__ float S_smem[Br * Bc]; __shared__ float smem_alpha[Br]; __shared__ float smem_m[Br]; __shared__ float smem_l[Br]; int tid = threadIdx.x; // Load Q tile int total_q = Br * D; for (int idx = tid; idx < total_q / 4; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (D / 4); int c = idx % (D / 4); Half4 val; if (q_start + r < seq_len) { val = *reinterpret_cast<const Half4*>(q_ptr + r * D + c * 4); } else { // zero initialize val.x = make_half2(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f)); val.y = make_half2(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f)); } *reinterpret_cast<Half4*>(Q_smem + r * D + c * 4) = val; } __syncthreads(); int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE; int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE; int row = warp_id * (Br / 4) + lane / 2; int d_start = (lane % 2) * (D / 2); bool is_leader = (lane % 2 == 0); float o_reg[D/2]; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < D/2; ++d) o_reg[d] = 0.0f; float m = -INFINITY; float l = 0.0f; int num_kv_tiles = (seq_len + Bc - 1) / Bc; for (int tile_kv = 0; tile_kv < num_kv_tiles; ++tile_kv) { int kv_start = tile_kv * Bc; // Load K tile int total_kv = Bc * D; // zero pad first for (int idx = tid; idx < total_kv / 4; idx += blockDim.x) { Half4 z; z.x = make_half2(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f)); z.y = make_half2(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f)); *reinterpret_cast<Half4*>(KV_smem + idx * 4) = z; } __syncthreads(); for (int idx = tid; idx < total_kv / 4; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (D / 4); int c = idx % (D / 4); if (kv_start + r < seq_len) { Half4 val = *reinterpret_cast<const Half4*>(k_ptr + (kv_start + r) * D + c * 4); // Softmax for this tile if (is_leader) { float m_ij = -INFINITY; #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < Bc; ++j) { float s = S_smem[row * Bc + j]; if (s > m_ij) m_ij = s; } float m_new = fmaxf(m, m_ij); float alpha = expf(m - m_new); smem_alpha[row] = alpha; smem_m[row] = m_new; float l_new = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < Bc; ++j) { float p = expf(S_smem[row * Bc + j] - m_new); S_smem[row * Bc + j] = p; l_new += p; } l = l * alpha + l_new; smem_l[row] = l; m = m_new; } __syncthreads(); // Apply alpha to O and update m, l for all threads float alpha = smem_alpha[row]; float m_new = smem_m[row]; float l_new = smem_l[row]; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < D/2; ++d) { o_reg[d] *= alpha; } m = m_new; l = l_new; // Load V tile for (int idx = tid; idx < total_kv / 4; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / (D / 4); int c = idx % (D / 4); float p = S_smem[row * Bc + j]; const half* v_row = KV_smem + j * D + d_start; #pragma unroll 16 for (int d = 0; d < D/2; d += 4) { float v0,v1,v2,v3; load_half4(v_row + d, v0, v1, v2, v3); o_reg[d+0] += p * v0; o_reg[d+1] += p * v1; o_reg[d+2] += p * v2; o_reg[d+3] += p * v3; } } } __syncthreads(); } // Write output if (q_start + row < seq_len) { float inv_l = 1.0f / smem_l[row]; half* out_row = out_ptr + row * D + d_start; #pragma unroll 16 for (int d = 0; d < D/2; d += 4) { float o0 = o_reg[d+0] * inv_l; float o1 = o_reg[d+1] * inv_l; float o2 = o_reg[d+2] * inv_l; float o3 = o_reg[d+3] * inv_l; Half4 h4; h4.x = make_half2(__float2half_rn(o0), __float2half_rn(o1)); h4.y = make_half2(__float2half_rn(o2), __float2half_rn(o3)); *reinterpret_cast<Half4*>(out_row + d) = h4; } } } torch::Tensor gqa_forward(torch::Tensor q, torch::Tensor k, torch::Tensor v) { int batch = q.size(0); int num_heads = q.size(1); int seq_len = q.size(2); int head_size = q.size(3); int num_kv_heads = k.size(1); TORCH_CHECK(q.scalar_type() == at::kHalf, "q must be half"); TORCH_CHECK(k.scalar_type() == at::kHalf, "k must be half"); TORCH_CHECK(v.scalar_type() == at::kHalf, "v must be half"); TORCH_CHECK(head_size == 128, "head_size must be 128"); float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)head_size); auto out = torch::empty_like(q); const half* q_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const half*>(q.data_ptr<at::Half>()); const half* k_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const half*>(k.data_ptr<at::Half>()); const half* v_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const half*>(v.data_ptr<at::Half>()); half* out_ptr = reinterpret_cast<half*>(out.data_ptr<at::Half>()); dim3 grid(batch * num_heads, (seq_len + 64 - 1) / 64); dim3 block(128); if (head_size == 128) { fused_gqa_kernel<128, 64, 64><<<grid, block>>>( q_ptr, k_ptr, v_ptr, out_ptr, batch, num_heads, num_kv_heads, seq_len, scale); } else { TORCH_CHECK(false, "Unsupported head size"); } return out; } PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) { m.def("forward", &gqa_forward, "Fused GQA forward"); } ``` Wait, there is a bug: The zeroing loop for KV_smem before loading K uses `KV_smem + idx * 4`. Let's check the `load_half4` again: ``` inline __device__ void load_half4(const half* ptr, float& o0, float& o1, float& o2, float& o3) { half2 a = *reinterpret_cast<const half2*>(ptr); half2 b = *reinterpret_cast<const half2*>(ptr + 2); float2 fa = __half22float2(a); float2 fb = __half22float2(b); o0 = fa.x; o1 = fa.y; o2 = fb.x; o3 = fb.y; } ``` Is `__half22float2` correct? Yes. For store: ``` inline __device__ void store_half4(half* ptr, float i0, float i1, float i2, float i3) { float2 fa = make_float2(i0, i1); float2 fb = make_float2(i2, i3); *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(ptr) = __float22half2_rn(fa); *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(ptr + 2) = __float22half2_rn(fb); } ``` Is `__float22half2_rn` correct? It takes a `float2` and returns `half2`. Yes. Actually, there is `__floats2half2_rn(float a, float b)` which takes two floats. I think `__float22half2_rn` is the one taking `float2`. Let's verify. In CUDA, there are: - `__float2half2_rn(float f)`: broadcast float to both halves. - `__floats2half2_rn(float a, float b)`: convert two floats to half2. - `__float22half2_rn(float2 f)`: convert float2 to half2. Yes, `__float22half2_rn` is correct. Alternatively, we can use `__floats2half2_rn(i0, i1)` directly. That avoids `make_float2`. Let's use that: ``` *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(ptr) = __floats2half2_rn(i0, i1); *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(ptr + 2) = __floats2half2_rn(i2, i3); ``` This is simpler. But I'm not 100% sure of the argument order. Usually `__floats2half2_rn(float a, float b)` returns `half2` where `a` is the high half and `b` is the low half? Or `a` is `.x` and `b` is `.y`? Wait, there is confusion. Let's stick to `__float22half2_rn(make_float2(i0, i1))`. `make_float2` is unambiguous: `.x = i0`, `.y = i1`. `__float22half2_rn` takes `float2` and returns `half2` with `.x` from `.x` and `.y` from `.y`? The documentation says "convert both components of float2 to half precision". So ordering is preserved. That is safe. Let's use that. For zeroing: ``` half2 zero = __float22half2_rn(make_float2(0.0f, 0.0f)); ``` Wait, I realize storing to `out` with `store_half4` uses `rn` rounding. The reference might use `rne` (round nearest even). `__float22half2_rn` uses round-to-nearest-even. That's fine. Let's also check the `q_ptr` arithmetic. `q_ptr` is `const half*`. `q_ptr + r * D + c * 4` points to `half` element at offset `r*D + c*4`. Since `c * 4` is in elements, and `half` is 2 bytes, the byte offset is `(r*D + c*4) * sizeof(half)`. This is correct. The reinterpret cast to `const half2*` for loads: `(q_ptr + r * D + c * 4)` is aligned to 4 bytes if `r*D + c*4` is even. Since D=128 and c*4 is multiple of 4, `r*128 + c*4` is divisible by 4. Good. Now, we need to be careful about `blockIdx.x / num_heads`. Since grid.x = batch * num_heads = 128. `blockIdx.x / num_heads` gives batch index (0..3). `blockIdx.x % num_heads` gives head (0..31). Good. Wait, what if `num_heads` is not divisible by `num_kv_heads`? The problem says NUM_HEADS=32, NUM_KV_HEADS=8, so group size = 4. `hq / (num_heads / num_kv_heads)` gives 0..7. Fine. Let's compile. I'll write the source string.
call → compile_kernel

Wait, 128 threads / 32 per warp = 4 warps. lane / 2 gives 16 values per warp. So row = warp_id * 16 + lane/2. That covers 0..63 exactly. d_start = (lane % 2) * 64. So half the threads in a warp handle d=0..63, the other half handle d=64..127. But for reduction via `__shfl_xor_sync` with mask 1, we need threads in pairs (even/odd lanes) to communicate. This works because lane 0 and 1 have same row, different d_start. They can shuffle to sum up their dot products. The leader (lane % 2 == 0) writes to s_smem. This design is sound. However, each thread only computes the dot product for half the dimensions (64 out of 128). It uses `__shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, acc, 1)` to sum with its partner. Then only the leader stores. This halves the compute work per thread pair but requires both to participate. The rest of the softmax logic is only done by leaders. But in the V multiplication, both threads in the pair need to read `ss` and update their own `o_reg`. The `ss` is in shared memory, accessible to all. This should work. Let me write the kernel cleanly: ```cuda #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <math.h> #define TILE_Q 64 #define TILE_KV 64 #define HEAD_DIM 128 #define WARP_SIZE 32 __global__ __launch_bounds__(128) void flash_gqa_kernel(...) { int b = blockIdx.x / num_heads; int hq = blockIdx.x % num_heads; int tq = blockIdx.y; int q0 = tq * TILE_Q; int hkv = hq / (num_heads / num_kv_heads); const half* q_ptr = q + ((b * num_heads + hq) * seq_len + q0) * HEAD_DIM; const half* k_ptr = k + ((b * num_kv_heads + hkv) * seq_len) * HEAD_DIM; const half* v_ptr = v + ((b * num_kv_heads + hkv) * seq_len) * HEAD_DIM; half* o_ptr = out + ((b * num_heads + hq) * seq_len + q0) * HEAD_DIM; __shared__ half q_smem[TILE_Q * HEAD_DIM]; __shared__ half kv_smem[TILE_KV * HEAD_DIM]; __shared__ float s_smem[TILE_Q * TILE_KV]; __shared__ float alpha_smem[TILE_Q]; __shared__ float m_smem[TILE_Q]; __shared__ float l_smem[TILE_Q]; int tid = threadIdx.x; // Load Q for (int i = tid; i < TILE_Q * HEAD_DIM / 2; i += blockDim.x) { int idx = i * 2; int r = idx / HEAD_DIM; int c = idx % HEAD_DIM; if (q0 + r < seq_len) { half2 tmp = *reinterpret_cast<const half2*>(q_ptr + r * HEAD_DIM + c); *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(q_smem + r * HEAD_DIM + c) = tmp; } else { *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(q_smem + r * HEAD_DIM + c) = make_half2(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f)); } } __syncthreads(); int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE; int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE; int row = warp_id * (TILE_Q / 4) + lane / 2; // 0..63 int d_base = (lane % 2) * (HEAD_DIM / 2); // 0 or 64 bool is_leader = (lane % 2 == 0); float o_reg[HEAD_DIM / 2]; for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM / 2; ++d) o_reg[d] = 0.0f; float m = -INFINITY; float l = 0.0f; int num_kv_tiles = (seq_len + TILE_KV - 1) / TILE_KV; for (int tk = 0; tk < num_kv_tiles; ++tk) { int k0 = tk * TILE_KV; // Zero KV for (int i = tid; i < TILE_KV * HEAD_DIM / 2; i += blockDim.x) { int idx = i * 2; *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(kv_smem + idx) = make_half2(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f)); } __syncthreads(); // Load K for (int i = tid; i < TILE_KV * HEAD_DIM / 2; i += blockDim.x) { int idx = i * 2; int r = idx / HEAD_DIM; int c = idx % HEAD_DIM; if (k0 + r < seq_len) { half2 tmp = *reinterpret_cast<const half2*>(k_ptr + (k0 + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(kv_smem + idx) = tmp; } } __syncthreads(); // Compute S row for (int j = 0; j < TILE_KV; ++j) { float acc = 0.0f; if (k0 + j < seq_len) { const half* q_row = q_smem + row * HEAD_DIM + d_base; const half* k_row = kv_smem + j * HEAD_DIM + d_base; #pragma unroll 8 for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM / 2; d += 2) { half2 hq2 = *reinterpret_cast<const half2*>(q_row + d * 2); // wait, d iterates over half2 elements half2 hk2 = *reinterpret_cast<const half2*>(k_row + d * 2); float2 fq = __half22float2(hq2); float2 fk = __half22float2(hk2); acc += fq.x * fk.x + fq.y * fk.y; } } else { acc = -INFINITY; } acc += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, acc, 1); if (is_leader) { s_smem[row * TILE_KV + j] = acc * scale; } } __syncthreads(); // Softmax if (is_leader) { float m_local = -INFINITY; for (int j = 0; j < TILE_KV; ++j) { float s = s_smem[row * TILE_KV + j]; if (s > m_local) m_local = s; } float m_new = fmaxf(m, m_local); float a = expf(m - m_new); alpha_smem[row] = a; m_smem[row] = m_new; float l_new = 0.0f; for (int j = 0; j < TILE_KV; ++j) { float p = expf(s_smem[row * TILE_KV + j] - m_new); s_smem[row * TILE_KV + j] = p; l_new += p; } l = l * a + l_new; l_smem[row] = l; m = m_new; } __syncthreads(); // Update O float a = alpha_smem[row]; m = m_smem[row]; l = l_smem[row]; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM / 2; ++d) o_reg[d] *= a; // Load V for (int i = tid; i < TILE_KV * HEAD_DIM / 2; i += blockDim.x) { int idx = i * 2; int r = idx / HEAD_DIM; int c = idx % HEAD_DIM; if (k0 + r < seq_len) { half2 tmp = *reinterpret_cast<const half2*>(v_ptr + (k0 + r) * HEAD_DIM + c); *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(kv_smem + idx) = tmp; } } __syncthreads(); if (k0 < seq_len) { for (int j = 0; j < TILE_KV; ++j) { if (k0 + j >= seq_len) break; float p = s_smem[row * TILE_KV + j]; const half* v_row = kv_smem + j * HEAD_DIM + d_base; #pragma unroll 8 for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM / 2; d += 2) { half2 hv = *reinterpret_cast<const half2*>(v_row + d * 2); float2 fv = __half22float2(hv); o_reg[d + 0] += p * fv.x; o_reg[d + 1] += p * fv.y; } } } __syncthreads(); } if (q0 + row < seq_len) { float inv_l = 1.0f / l_smem[row]; half* o_row = o_ptr + row * HEAD_DIM + d_base; #pragma unroll 8 for (int d = 0; d < HEAD_DIM / 2; d += 2) { float2 out_vec = make_float2(o_reg[d+0] * inv_l, o_reg[d+1] * inv_l); *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(o_row + d * 2) = __float22half2_rn(out_vec); } } } ``` Wait, I made an index calculation error: `d` ranges from 0 to HEAD_DIM/2 - 1 = 63. `d_base` is 0 or 64. If lane 0 is valid and lane 1 is invalid (same row), lane 1 sends garbage to lane 0 during `__shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, acc, 1)`. Then lane 0 adds garbage to its correct sum. Here is my plan for the kernel: ```cuda #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <math.h> #define D 128 #define Br 64 #define Bc 64 #define WARP_SIZE 32 __global__ __launch_bounds__(128) void attn_kernel(const half* q, const half* k, const half* v, half* out, int batch, int num_heads, int num_kv_heads, int seq_len, float scale) { int b = blockIdx.x / num_heads; } } } torch::Tensor fwd(torch::Tensor q, torch::Tensor k, torch::Tensor v) { int B = q.size(0); int H = q.size(1); int L = q.size(2); int Dv = q.size(3); int Hkv = k.size(1); auto out = torch::empty_like(q); float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)Dv); const half* qp = reinterpret_cast<const half*>(q.data_ptr<at::Half>()); const half* kp = reinterpret_cast<const half*>(k.data_ptr<at::Half>()); const half* vp = reinterpret_cast<const half*>(v.data_ptr<at::Half>()); half* op = reinterpret_cast<half*>(out.data_ptr<at::Half>()); dim3 grid(B * H, (L + Br - 1) / Br); dim3 block(128); attn_kernel<<<grid, block>>>(qp, kp, vp, op, B, H, Hkv, L, scale); return out; } PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) { m.def("forward", &fwd, "fwd"); Why? Because the dot product for valid j's loads from kv_smem. For invalid j's, we set acc = -INF directly. So we actually don't need to zero kv_smem before K load either! But I did it. It's okay. Wait, if I don't zero kv_smem before K load, and some positions are invalid, those threads won't read kv_smem (because they skip to else). So it's fine. Zeroing is unnecessary but safe. So I can remove the zeroing step to save a loop. But leaving it is fine. Let's refine the code and check for any compilation issues. I think `reinterpret_cast` on shared memory is okay for CUDA. One issue: the function signature uses `int num_kv_heads` but it's not a template parameter. That's fine. Another potential issue: division by zero in `1.0f / l` if `l` is 0. This can happen if `seq_len = 0`. But `seq_len` in the problem is 1024, so not an issue. If `seq_len` is 0, the reference would also be problematic. We can guard it but probably unnecessary. What if `l` stays 0 because seq_len is 0? Then `inv_l` is inf. But not relevant. Now, for `num_groups = num_heads / num_kv_heads`. `h / num_groups` must be integer. `num_heads` is 32, `num_kv_heads` is 8. So `num_groups` is 4. `hkv = h / 4`. This is correct. Now, a critical thing: when `num_heads / num_kv_heads` doesn't divide evenly? The problem says it does. Okay, I think the code is good. I'll write the final Python wrapper. One more optimization for H100: we can use `__stcs` and `__ldcs`? Not needed. I will avoid explicit `-gencode` to prevent potential issues and let PyTorch detect the architecture. I'll use `extra_cuda_cflags=["-O2", "--use_fast_math"]` for the first compile attempt to see if it passes. If it compiles and is correct, I can try `-O3` later. But `-O2` might be fast enough. Actually, for such a small kernel, `-O2` vs `-O3` probably makes little difference. But `-O3` might vectorize better. Given my previous timeout with `-O3`, I'll try `-O2` now. But wait, maybe the timeout was caused by something else entirely. Let me just try. Wait, I just realized: `make_half2` might not compile because `half` is a CUDA built-in and `make_half2` is defined in `cuda_fp16.hpp`? Actually, `make_half2` is defined in `cuda_fp16.h`. It should be fine. Alternative: `__floats2half2_rn(0.0f, 0.0f)` creates a half2 from two floats. This is often safer. I'll use that. ```cuda *reinterpret_cast<half2*>(q_smem + idx) = __floats2half2_rn(0.0f, 0.0f); ``` Actually, `__floats2half2_rn` takes two floats. Yes. For `make_half2(__float2half(0.0f), __float2half(0.0f))`, it's also fine. I'll use `__floats2half2_rn` to be safe. Wait, let me check documentation. `__floats2half2_rn(float x, float y)` converts two floats to a half2 using round-to-nearest. Yes. And `__float22half2_rn(float2 f)` converts a float2 to half2. I'll use these. One more thing: `__shfl_xor_sync` is only available on sm_30+. H100 is sm_90. Fine. Let me write the final code block. Actually, for the Python wrapper, I need to handle lazy loading properly. I'll define `_mod = None` and `_get_mod()`. Hmm, I should also consider that `torch.utils.cpp_extension.load_inline` caches compiled extensions in `~/.cache/torch_extensions/...`. If the name is the same, it might reuse the cached one. But the tool environment might not persist the cache. Using a unique name each time is safer, but it means recompilation every time. I'll use `name="gqa_hopper_v1"` and keep it consistent.
